Let the input instance of
DS-HyperGraph-Node-to-Area-CA-using-H
be
((G,c),\mathcal{W},r,m)
, where
(G=(V,E),c\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}E\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{+})
is a hypergraph,
\mathcal{W}\subseteq 2^{V}
is a collection of subsets,
r\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathcal{W}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{+}
is the target connectivity function, and
m\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}V\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
is the degree-specification.
We consider the set function
R\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
defined by
R(X)\coloneqq\max\{r(W)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}W\in\mathcal{W},W\cap X=\emptyset\text{ or }W\subseteq X\}
for every non-empty proper subset
X\subseteq V
,
R(\emptyset)\coloneqq 0
, and
R(V)\coloneqq 0
.
Next, we consider the function
p\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
defined by
p(X)\coloneqq R(X)-d_{(G,c)}(X)
for every
X\subseteq V
. The function
p
is symmetric skew-supermodular by
Lemma

6.3
. Moreover, for a given hypergraph
(G_{0}=(V,E_{0}),c_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}E_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{+})
, and disjoint sets
S_{0},T_{0}\subseteq V
, and vector
y_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{V}
, the oracle
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{sc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}

((G_{0},c_{0}),S_{0},T_{0})
can be implemented to run in
\poly(|V|,|E|,|E_{0}|)
time.

We observe that a hypergraph
(H=(V,E_{H}),w\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}E_{H}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{+})
is a feasible solution for the input instance
((G,c),\mathcal{W},r,m)
of
DS-HyperGraph-Node-to-Area-CA-using-H
if and only if the hypergraph
(H,w)
is a feasible solution for the instance
(p,m)
of
DS-Sym-Skew-SupMod-StrongCover-using-H
.
By
Corollary

6.5
there exists an algorithm that returns a near-uniform hypergraph solution for
DS-Sym-Skew-SupMod-StrongCover-using-H
that runs in time
\poly(|V|)
using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{sc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
, where for each query to
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{sc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
, the hypergraph
(G_{0},c_{0})
used as input to the query has
O(|V|)
vertices and
O(|V|)
hyperedges. Thus, using our implementation of
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{sc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
from the first paragraph, this algorithm can be implemented to run in
\poly(|V|,|E|)
time.
∎

We note that a
simultaneous
version of
DS-HyperGraph-Node-to-Area-CA-using-H
can be defined (analogous to
DS-Hypergraph-CA-using-H
and
DS-Simul-Hypergraph-CA-using-H
) and
Corollary

6.3
can be applied to derive a strongly polynomial time algorithm for it. The optimization variants instead of the degree-specified variants can also be solved in strongly polynomial time. We refrain from stating the details in the interests of brevity.

6.4
Degree-constrained mixed-hypergraph global connectivity augmentation using hyperedges

The notion of mixed-hypergraph generalizes undirected graphs/hypergraphs and directed graphs/hypergraphs (e.g., see
[
30
]
).
A mixed-hypergraph
(G=(V,A),c\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}A\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{+})
consists of a finite set
V
of vertices and a set
A
of hyperarcs with weights
c(e)
for every
e\in A
, where each hyperarc
e\in A
is specified by an ordered tuple
(\text{tails}(e),\text{ heads}(e),\text{ heads-tails}(e))
satisfying
\text{tails}(e),\text{ heads}(e),\text{ heads-tails(e)}\subseteq V
with
\text{tails}(e)\cap\text{heads}(e)=\emptyset
,
\text{tails}(e)\cap\text{heads-tails}(e)=\emptyset
,
\text{heads}(e)\cap\text{heads-tails}(e)=\emptyset
,
\text{tails}(e)\cup\text{heads-tails}(e)\neq\emptyset
and
\text{heads}(e)\cup\text{heads-tails}(e)\neq\emptyset
.
For an undirected hypergraph
(H=(V,E_{H}),w_{H}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}E_{H}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{+})
, we define an associated mixed-hypergraph
(\overrightarrow{H}=(V,\overrightarrow{E_{H}}),w_{\overrightarrow{H}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\overrightarrow{E_{H}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{+})
where for every
hyperedge
e_{H}\in E_{H}
, we introduce a hyperarc
e_{\overrightarrow{H}}\coloneqq(\text{tails}(e_{\overrightarrow{H}})=\emptyset,\text{ heads}(e_{\overrightarrow{H}})=\emptyset,\text{ heads-tails}(e_{\overrightarrow{H}})=e_{H})
into
\overrightarrow{E_{H}}
with weight
w_{\overrightarrow{H}}(e_{\overrightarrow{H}})=w_{H}(e_{H})
.
For a set
X\subseteq V
, we define
\delta_{G}^{in}(X)\coloneqq\{e\in A\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}(\text{heads}(e)\cup\text{heads-tails}(e))\cap A\neq\emptyset,(\text{tails}(e)\cup\text{heads-tails}(e))\cap(V\setminus A)\neq\emptyset\}
and the function
d^{in}_{(G,w)}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
defined by
d^{in}_{(G,w)}(X)\coloneqq\sum_{e\in\delta_{G}^{in}(X)}w(e)
for every
X\subseteq V
.
For distinct vertices
u,v\in V
, we define
\lambda_{(G,w)}(u,v)\coloneqq\min\{d^{in}_{(G,w)}(X)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}v\in X\subseteq V-\{u\}\}
. We note that
\lambda_{(G,w)}(u,v)
is not necessarily equal to
\lambda_{(G,w)}(v,u)
for distinct vertices
u,v\in V
.
Let
r\in V
be a designated root vertex.
If
\lambda_{(G,w)}(v,r)\geq k
and
\lambda_{(G,w)}(r,v)\geq\ell
for every
v\in V\setminus\{r\}
, then the mixed-hypergraph is said to be
(k,\ell)
-rooted-arc-connected.

We consider the degree-constrained mixed-hypergraph global connectivity augmentation using hyperedges problem (denoted
DC-Mixed-HyperGraph-Global-CA-using-H
): the input is a degree-bound function
m\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}V\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, a mixed-hypergraph
(G=(V,A),c\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}A\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{+})
, a designated root vertex
r\in V
, and values
k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
.
The goal is to verify if there exists an undirected hypergraph
(H=(V,E_{H}),w\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}E_{H}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{+})

such that

b_{(H,w)}(u)\leq m(u)
for every
u\in V

and the mixed-hypergraph
(G+\overrightarrow{H},c+w_{\overrightarrow{H}})
is
(k,\ell)
-rooted-arc-connected, where

(G+\overrightarrow{H}=(V,E_{G+\overrightarrow{H}}),c+w_{\overrightarrow{H}})
is the mixed-hypergraph on vertex set
V
and hyperarc set
E_{G+\overrightarrow{H}}\coloneqq A\cup\overrightarrow{E_{H}}
with the weight of every
hyperarc
e\in A\cap\overrightarrow{E_{H}}
being
c(e)+w_{\overrightarrow{H}}(e)
, the weight of every hyperarc
e\in A\setminus\overrightarrow{E_{H}}
being
c(e)
and the weight of every hyperarc
e\in\overrightarrow{E_{H}}\setminus A
being
w_{\overrightarrow{H}}(e)
; moreover, the goal involves finding such a hypergraph if it exists.
Bernáth and Király
[
8
]
introduced this problem and showed a complete characterization for the existence of a feasible solution. They also showed that for feasible instances, there exists a feasible solution that is also near-uniform. Our results imply a strongly polynomial-time algorithm for this problem. The next lemma shows that the appropriate function maximization oracle can be implemented in strongly polynomial time. We defer the proof of the lemma to
Appendix

A
. We state and prove the main result of the section below the lemma.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
g\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
be a submodular function,
r\in V
, and
k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Let
p\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
be the function defined by

\displaystyle p(X)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }X=\emptyset\text{ or }X=V,\\
k-g(X)&\text{ if }\emptyset\neq X\subseteq V\setminus\{r\},\text{ and}\\
\ell-g(X)&\text{ if }r\in X\subsetneq V,\end{cases}

and let
p_{sym}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
be the function defined by
p_{sym}(X)\coloneqq\max\{p(X),p(V\setminus X)\}
for every
X\subseteq V
. Then we have that

1.

the function
p
is symmetric skew-supermodular, and

2.

given access to the evaluation oracle of the function
g
, a hypergraph
(G_{0}=(V,E_{0}),c_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}E_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{+})
, disjoint sets
S_{0},T_{0}\subseteq V
, and vector
y_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{V}
, the oracle
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{sc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}

((G_{0},c_{0}),S_{0},T_{0},y_{0})
can be computed in
\poly(|V|,|E_{0}|)
time.

Theorem 6.2
.

There exists a strongly polynomial time algorithm to solve
DC-Mixed-HyperGraph-Global-CA-using-H
. Moreover if the instance is feasible, then the algorithm returns a solution hypergraph that is near-uniform and contains
O(n)
hyperedges, where
n
is the number of vertices in the input hypergraph.

Proof.

Let the input instance of
DC-Mixed-HyperGraph-Global-CA-using-H
be
((G,c),r,m,k,\ell)
, where
(G=(V,A),w\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}A\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{+})
is a mixed hypergraph,
r\in V
is the designated root vertex,
m\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}V\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
is the degree-bound function, and
k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
.
We note that the function
d^{in}_{(G,c)}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
is submodular.
Consider the function
p\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
defined by

\displaystyle p(X)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }X=\emptyset\text{ or }X=V,\\
k-d^{in}_{(G,c)}(X)&\text{ if }\emptyset\neq X\subseteq V\setminus\{r\},\text{ and}\\
\ell-d^{in}_{(G,c)}(X)&\text{ if }r\in X\subsetneq V.\end{cases}

We define
p_{sym}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
as
p_{sym}(X)\coloneqq\max\{p(X),p(V\setminus X)\}
for every
X\subseteq V
. The function
p
is symmetric skew-supermodular by
Lemma

6.4
. Moreover, for a given hypergraph
(G_{0}=(V,E_{0}),c_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}E_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{+})
, and disjoint sets
S_{0},T_{0}\subseteq V
, and vector
y_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{V}
, the oracle
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{sc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}

((G_{0},c_{0}),S_{0},T_{0})
can be implemented to run in
\poly(|V|,|A|,|E_{0}|)
time—we note that the evaluation oracle for the function
d^{in}_{(G_{0},c_{0})}
can be implemented in
\poly(|V|,|A|)
time.

We observe that a hypergraph
(H=(V,E_{H}),w\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}E_{H}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{+})
is a feasible solution for the input instance
((G,c),r,m,k,\ell)
of
DC-Mixed-HyperGraph-Global-CA-using-H
if and only if the hypergraph
(H,w)
is a feasible solution for the instance
(p,m)
of
DS-Sym-Skew-SupMod-StrongCover-using-H
.
By
Corollary

6.2
there exists an algorithm that returns a near-uniform hypergraph solution for
DS-Sym-Skew-SupMod-StrongCover-using-H
that runs in time
\poly(|V|)
using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{sc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
, where for each query to
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{sc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
, the hypergraph
(G_{0},c_{0})
used as input to the query has
O(|V|)
vertices and
O(|V|)
hyperedges. Thus, using our implementation of
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{sc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
from the first paragraph, this algorithm can be implemented to run in
\poly(|V|,|A|)
time.
∎

We note that a
simultaneous
version of
DC-Mixed-HyperGraph-Global-CA-using-H
can be defined (analogous to
DS-Hypergraph-CA-using-H
and
DS-Simul-Hypergraph-CA-using-H
) and
Corollary

6.3
can be applied to derive a polynomial-time algorithm for it. We refrain from stating the details in the interests of brevity.

7
Conclusion

The central theme of this work is showing that certain hypergraph network design problems admit solution hypergraphs with polynomial number of hyperedges and moreover, can be solved in strongly polynomial time.
Our algorithms also return solution hypergraphs that are near-uniform.
Our results are for certain abstract function cover problems but they have numerous applications; in particular, they enable the first strongly polynomial time algorithms for (i) degree-specified hypergraph connectivity augmentation using hyperedges, (ii) degree-specified hypergraph node-to-area connectivity augmentation using hyperedges, and (iii) degree-constrained mixed-hypergraph global connectivity augmentation using hyperedges.
Previous best-known run-time for these problems were pseudo-polynomial. We believe that the abstract function cover problems might find more applications in the future.

The abstract function cover problems were introduced by Szigeti
[
58
]
and Bernáth and Király
[
8
]
. Previously known algorithms for these problems were pseudo-polynomial and there exist instances on which these algorithms necessarily take exponential time. Our main contributions are modifying these algorithms and introducing suitable potential functions to bound the recursion depth of the modified algorithms.

Our work raises two natural open questions.
Algorithmic question:
Is it possible to solve
DS-Hypergraph-CA-using-H
in near-linear time?
Structural question:
For feasible instances of
DS-Hypergraph-CA-using-H
, does there exist a solution hypergraph whose
size
is linear in the number of vertices? We define the size of a hypergraph to be the sum of the sizes of the hyperedges (and not simply the number of hyperedges). Our results show that
there exists a solution hypergraph in which
the number of hyperedges is linear and hence, the size of such a solution hypergraph is quadratic in the number of vertices. We believe that an affirmative answer to the algorithmic question would also imply an affirmative answer to the structural question. On the other hand, answering the structural question would be a helpful stepping stone towards the algorithmic question. We note that recent results have shown that
DS-Graph-CA-using-E
for the case of uniform requirement function can be solved in near-linear time
[
14
,
15
,
13
]
.

Appendix A
Maximization oracles for functions arising in applications

In this section, we show that certain set functions
p\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
arising in our applications are skew-supermodular and admit efficient algorithms to implement

{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{sc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
.
We prove Lemmas
6.3
, and
6.4
.
These lemmas have been observed in the literature before. We include their proofs for the sake of completeness.

See
6.3

Proof.

For ease of notation, let
p\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=p_{(G,c,r)}

We note that the function
p
was shown to be symmetric skew-supermodular by Bernath and Kiraly
[
8
]
. We show the second part of the lemma.
Let the input to the oracle be the hypergraph
(G_{0}=(V,E_{0}),c_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}E_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{+})
, disjoint subsets
S_{0},T_{0}\subseteq V
, and vector
y_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{V}
. For a subset
Z\subseteq V
, we use
y_{0}(Z)\coloneqq\sum_{z\in Z}y_{0}(z)
.
Let
\mathcal{W}_{S_{0}}\coloneqq\{W\in\mathcal{W}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}W\cap S_{0}=\emptyset\}
and
\mathcal{W}_{T_{0}}\coloneqq\{W\in\mathcal{W}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}W\cap T_{0}=\emptyset\}
.
We define the following:

1.

For every
W\in\mathcal{W}_{T_{0}}
, let

\lambda_{1}(W)\coloneqq\min\left\{d_{(G,c)}(Z)+d_{(G_{0},c_{0})}(Z)-y_{0}(Z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}S_{0}+W\subseteq Z\subseteq V-T_{0}\right\}

and
Z_{1}^{W}
be a set that achieves the minimum.

2.

For every
W\in\mathcal{W}_{S_{0}}
, let

\lambda_{2}(W)\coloneqq\min\left\{d_{(G,c)}(Z)+d_{(G_{0},c_{0})}(Z)-y_{0}(Z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}S_{0}\subseteq Z\subseteq V-T_{0}-W\right\}

and
Z_{2}^{W}
be a set that achieves the minimum.

For
W\in\mathcal{W}_{S_{0}}\cup\mathcal{W}_{T_{0}}
, let

\displaystyle\lambda(W)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{cases}\min\{\lambda_{1}(W),\lambda_{2}(W)\}\text{ if }W\in\mathcal{W}_{S_{0}}\cap\mathcal{W}_{T_{0}}\\
\lambda_{1}(W)\text{ if }W\in\mathcal{W}_{T_{0}}\\
\lambda_{2}(W)\text{ if }W\in\mathcal{W}_{S_{0}}.\end{cases}

\displaystyle Z^{W}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{cases}\arg\min\{d_{(G,c)}(Z_{1}^{W})+b_{(G_{0},c_{0})}(Z_{1}^{W})-y_{0}(Z_{1}^{W}),d_{(G,c)}(Z_{2}^{W})+b_{(G_{0},c_{0})}(Z_{2}^{W})-y_{0}(Z_{2}^{W})\}\text{ if }W\in\mathcal{W}_{S_{0}}\cap\mathcal{W}_{T_{0}}\\
Z_{1}^{W}\text{ if }W\in\mathcal{W}_{T_{0}}\\
Z_{2}^{W}\text{ if }W\in\mathcal{W}_{S_{0}}.\end{cases}

For every
W\in\mathcal{W}_{S_{0}}\cup\mathcal{W}_{T_{0}}
, the value
\lambda(W)
and the set
Z^{W}
can be computed in
\poly(|V|,|E|,|E_{0}|)
time via submodular minimization. For ease of notation, let
R\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=p+d_{(G,c)}
. Now, we observe that

\displaystyle\max

\displaystyle\left\{p(Z)-d_{(G_{0},c_{0})}(Z)+y_{0}(Z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}S_{0}\subseteq Z\subseteq V-T_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle=\max\left\{R(Z)-d_{(G,c)}(Z)-d_{(G_{0},c_{0})}(Z)+y_{0}(Z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}S_{0}\subseteq Z\subseteq V-T_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle=\max\left\{r(W)-\lambda(W)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}W\in\mathcal{W}_{S_{0}}\cup\mathcal{W}_{T_{0}}\right\}.

The RHS problem can be solved in
\poly(|V|,|E|,|E_{0}|)
time by iterating over all
W\in\mathcal{W}_{S_{0}}\cup\mathcal{W}_{T_{0}}
and returning a set
W
for which the objective function
r(W)-\lambda(W)
is maximum. Let
W\in\mathcal{W}_{S_{0}}\cup\mathcal{W}_{T_{0}}
be a set which achieves the maximum in the RHS problem. Then, we observe that the set
Z^{W}
is the maximizer of the LHS problem. Thus, it suffices to return
Z^{W}
and the value
p(Z^{W})=R(Z^{W})-d_{(G_{0},c_{0})}(Z^{W})
which can also be computed in
\poly(|V|,|E|,|E_{0}|)
time. This completes the proof that
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{wc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
can be implemented in
\poly(|V|,|E|,|E_{0}|)
time.
∎

See
6.4

Proof.

We note that the function
p
was shown to be symmetric skew-supermodular by Bernath and Kiraly
[
8
]
. We show the second part of the lemma.
Let the input to the oracle be the hypergraph
(G_{0}=(V,E_{0}),c_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}E_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{+})
, disjoint subsets
S_{0},T_{0}\subseteq V
, and vector
y_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{V}
.
For a subset
Z\subseteq V
, we use
y_{0}(Z)\coloneqq\sum_{z\in Z}y_{0}(z)
.
Let
p_{1},p_{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V\setminus\{r\}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
be functions defined by

\displaystyle p_{1}(X)

\displaystyle\coloneqq p(X)-d_{(G_{0},c_{0})}(X)-y_{0}(X)\ \forall\ X\subseteq V\setminus\{r\}\ \text{ and}

\displaystyle p_{2}(X)

\displaystyle\coloneqq p(X\cup\{r\})-d_{(G_{0},c_{0})}(X\cup\{r\})-y_{0}(X\cup\{r\})\ \forall\ X\subseteq V\setminus\{r\}.

Then, the functions
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
are intersecting supermodular since the function
g
and the coverage function
b_{(G_{0},c_{0})}
are submodular (see
[
30
]
for the definition of intersecting submodular functions). For an intersecting submodular function
f\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
and given disjoint subsets
S,T\subseteq V
using the evaluation oracle for
f
, we can find an optimum solution to
\min\{f(X)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}S\subseteq X\subseteq V-T\}
in strongly polynomial time
[
30
]
.
Consequently, using access to the evaluation oracle of the function
g
, we can find sets
Z_{1},Z_{2}\subseteq(V\setminus\{r\})-T_{0}
such that
S_{0}\subseteq Z_{1},Z_{2}
and
p_{1}(Z_{1})\geq p_{1}(X)
and
p_{2}(Z_{2})\geq p_{2}(X)
for every
X\subseteq V\setminus\{r\}
via submodular minimization in
\poly(|V|,|E_{0}|)
. Let
Z\coloneqq\arg\min\{p(Z_{1}),p(Z_{2}+r)\}
. Then, we observe that the set
Z
is an optimum solution to

\max\left\{p(Z)-d_{(G_{0},c_{0})}(Z)+y_{0}(Z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}S_{0}\subseteq Z\subseteq V-T_{0}\right\}.

Hence, it suffices to return
Z
and its function value
p(Z)
which can be computed in
\poly(|V|,|E_{0}|)
time using access to the evaluation oracle of the function
g
.
∎

Appendix B
Properties of Laminar Families and Skew-Supermodular Functions

In this section, we prove
Lemma

3.1
,
Lemma

3.2
,
Lemma

5.13
and
Lemma

5.14
.

B.1
Cumulative Minimal Maximizer Family Across Function Sequences

We recall that for a function
p\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
, the family of minimal
p
-maximizers is denoted by
{\mathcal{F}}_{p}
. We now restate and prove
Lemma

3.1
which says that this family is disjoint if
p
is a skew-supermodular function.

See
3.1

Proof.

By way of contradiction, let
X,Y\in{\mathcal{F}}_{p}
be distinct sets such that
X\cap Y=\emptyset
. We note that
X-Y,Y-X\not=\emptyset
as otherwise either
X\subset Y
or
Y\subset X
which contradicts the minimality of the sets
Y
and
X
respectively. We consider two cases based on the behavior of the function
p
on the sets
X,Y
. First, suppose that
p(X)+p(Y)\leq p(X\cup Y)+p(X\cap Y)
. Then we have that
p(X)+p(Y)\leq p(X\cup Y)+p(X\cap Y)<p(X)+p(Y),
a contradiction. Here, we have
p(X\cup Y)\leq p(X)
since
X
is a
p
-maximizer and
p(X\cap Y)<p(Y)
since
Y
is a minimal
p
-maximizer. Next suppose that
p(X)+p(Y)\leq p(X-Y)+p(X-Y)
. Then we have that
p(X)+p(Y)\leq p(X-Y)+p(X-Y)<p(X)+p(Y),
a contradiction. Here, the inequality is because
p(X-Y)<p(X)
and
p(Y-X)<p(Y)
since the sets
X
and
Y
are minimal
p
-maximizers.
∎

Let
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
be a positive integer. Let
p_{1},p_{2},\ldots,p_{\ell}
be a sequence of skew-supermodular functions defined over a ground set
V
. We recall that the family
{\mathcal{F}}_{p_{\leq i}}
for every
i\in[\ell]
is defined as follows:

{\mathcal{F}}_{p_{\leq i}}\coloneqq\bigcup_{j\in[i]}{\mathcal{F}}_{p_{i}}.

We now restate and prove
Lemma

3.2
which says that the family
{\mathcal{F}}_{p_{\leq i}}
is laminar for a specific sequence of functions.
See
3.2

Proof.

We first show a useful claim that establishes a relationship between two arbitrary functions in the sequence
p_{1},p_{2},\ldots p_{\ell}
.

Claim B.1
.

Let
a,b\in[\ell]
with
a<b
. Then, we have that

p_{b}(X)=\max\left\{p_{a}\left(X\uplus\biguplus_{j\in[a,b-1]}R_{j}\right)-\sum_{j\in[a,b-1]}g_{j}\left(X\uplus\biguplus_{k\in[j+1,b-1]}R_{k}\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}R_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{j}\text{ for }j\in[a,b-1]\right\}.

Proof.

We prove the claim by induction on
b-a
. The claim for the base case
b=a+1
is because
p_{a+1}={p_{a}}/_{\mathcal{Z}_{a}}-g_{a}
. We consider the inductive case
b>a+1
. We have the following:

\displaystyle p_{b}(X)

\displaystyle=\max\left\{p_{b-1}\left(X\uplus R_{b-1}\right)-g_{b-1}\left(X\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}R_{b-1}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{b-1}\right\}

\displaystyle=\max\left\{p_{a}\left(X\uplus\biguplus_{j\in[a,b-1]}R_{j}\right)-\sum_{j\in[a,b-1]}g_{j}\left(X\uplus\biguplus_{k\in[j+1,b-1]}R_{k}\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}R_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{j}\text{ for }j\in[a,b-1]\right\}.

Here, the first equality is because
p_{b}={p_{b-1}}/_{\mathcal{Z}_{b-1}}-g_{b-1}
and the second equality is by the inductive hypothesis.
∎

By way of contradiction, suppose that the family
{\mathcal{F}}_{p_{\leq\ell}}
is not laminar. Then, there exist sets
A,B\in{\mathcal{F}}_{p_{\leq\ell}}
such that
A-B,B-A,A\cap B\not=\emptyset
. Let
a\coloneqq\min\{i\in[\ell]\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}A\in{\mathcal{F}}_{p_{a}}\}
and
b\coloneqq\min\{i\in[\ell]\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}B\in{\mathcal{F}}_{p_{b}}\}
. We have that
a\not=b
as otherwise the sets
A,B
are disjoint by
Lemma

3.1
, contradicting our choice of the sets
A,B
. We assume without loss of generality that
a<b
. We note that
B\subseteq V_{a}
since
B\subseteq V_{b}\subseteq V_{a}
. By
B.1
, we have that

p_{b}(B)=\max\left\{p_{a}\left(B\uplus\biguplus_{j\in[a,b-1]}R_{j}\right)-\sum_{j\in[a,b-1]}g_{j}\left(B\uplus\biguplus_{k\in[j+1,b-1]}R_{k}\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}R_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{j}\text{ for }j\in[a,b-1]\right\}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle K_{p_{a}}+K_{p_{b}}

\displaystyle=p_{a}(A)+p_{b}(B)

\displaystyle=p_{a}(A)+\max\left\{p_{a}\left(B\uplus\biguplus_{j\in[a,b-1]}R_{j}\right)-\sum_{j\in[a,b-1]}g_{j}\left(B\uplus\biguplus_{k\in[j+1,b-1]}R_{k}\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}R_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{j}\text{ for }j\in[a,b-1]\right\}

\displaystyle=p_{a}(A)+p_{a}\left(B\uplus\biguplus_{j\in[a,b-1]}\tilde{R}_{j}\right)-\sum_{j\in[a,b-1]}g_{j}\left(B\uplus\biguplus_{k\in[j+1,b-1]}\tilde{R}_{k}\right),

for some sets
\tilde{R}_{k}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{k}
for every
k\in[a,b-1]
. For notational convenience, we denote
S\coloneqq B\uplus\biguplus_{j\in[a,b-1]}\tilde{R}_{j}
. Now, we consider two cases based on the behavior of the function
p_{a}
at the sets
A,S
.

First, we consider the case where the function
p_{a}
is locally supermodular at the sets
A
and
S
, i.e.
p_{a}(A)+p_{a}(S)\leq p_{a}(A\cup S)+p_{a}(A\cap S)
. Then we have the following:

\displaystyle K_{p_{a}}+K_{p_{b}}

\displaystyle=p_{a}(A)+p_{a}(S)-\sum_{j\in[a,b-1]}g_{j}\left(B\uplus\biguplus_{k\in[j+1,b-1]}\tilde{R}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq p_{a}(A\cup S)+p_{a}(A\cap S)-\sum_{j\in[a,b-1]}g_{j}\left(B\uplus\biguplus_{k\in[j+1,b-1]}\tilde{R}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq K_{p_{a}}+p_{a}(A\cap S)-\sum_{j\in[a,b-1]}g_{j}\left(\left(A\cap B\right)\uplus\biguplus_{k\in[j+1,b-1]}\tilde{R}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=K_{p_{a}}+p_{a}\left(A\cap\left(B\uplus\biguplus_{j\in[a,b-1]}\tilde{R}_{j}\right)\right)-\sum_{j\in[a,b-1]}g_{j}\left(\left(A\cap B\right)\uplus\biguplus_{k\in[j+1,b-1]}\tilde{R}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=K_{p_{a}}+p_{a}\left(\left(A\cap B\right)\uplus\left(A\cap\biguplus_{j\in[a,b-1]}\tilde{R}_{j}\right)\right)-\sum_{j\in[a,b-1]}g_{j}\left(\left(A\cap B\right)\uplus\biguplus_{k\in[j+1,b-1]}\tilde{R}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq K_{p_{a}}+\max\left\{p_{a}\left((A\cap B)\uplus\biguplus_{j\in[a,b-1]}R_{j}\right)-\sum_{j\in[a,b-1]}g_{j}\left((A\cap B)\uplus\biguplus_{k\in[j+1,b-1]}R_{k}\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}R_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{j}\text{ for }j\in[a,b-1]\right\}

\displaystyle=K_{p_{a}}+p_{b}(A\cap B)

\displaystyle\leq K_{p_{a}}+K_{p_{b}}.

Here, the second inequality is by the monotonicity of the functions
g_{j}
for every
j\in[\ell]
, while the last equality is by
B.1
.
Thus, all inequalities are equations and we have that
p_{b}(A\cap B)=K_{p_{b}}
, contradicting minimality of the set
B\in{\mathcal{F}}_{p_{b}}
.

Next, we consider the case where the function
p_{a}
is locally negamodular at the sets
A
and
S
, i.e.
p_{a}(A)+p_{a}(S)\leq p_{a}(A-S)+p_{a}(S-A)
. Then we have the following:

\displaystyle K_{p_{a}}+K_{p_{b}}

\displaystyle=p_{a}(A)+p_{a}(S)-\sum_{j\in[a,b-1]}g_{j}\left(B\uplus\biguplus_{k\in[j+1,b-1]}\overline{R}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq p_{a}(A-S)+p_{a}(S-A)-\sum_{j\in[a,b-1]}g_{j}\left(B\uplus\biguplus_{k\in[j+1,b-1]}\overline{R}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq K_{p_{a}}+p_{a}(S-A)-\sum_{j\in[a,b-1]}g_{j}\left(\left(B-A\right)\uplus\biguplus_{k\in[j+1,b-1]}\overline{R}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=K_{p_{a}}+p_{a}\left(\left(B\uplus\biguplus_{j\in[a,b-1]}\overline{R}_{j}\right)-A\right)-\sum_{j\in[a,b-1]}g_{j}\left(\left(B-A\right)\uplus\biguplus_{k\in[j+1,b-1]}\overline{R}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=K_{p_{a}}+p_{a}\left(\left(B-A\right)\uplus\left(\biguplus_{j\in[a,b-1]}\overline{R}_{j}\right)-A\right)-\sum_{j\in[a,b-1]}g_{j}\left(\left(B-A\right)\uplus\biguplus_{k\in[j+1,b-1]}\overline{R}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq K_{p_{a}}+\max\left\{p_{a}\left((B-A)\uplus\biguplus_{j\in[a,b-1]}R_{j}\right)-\sum_{j\in[a,b-1]}g_{j}\left((B-A)\uplus\biguplus_{k\in[j+1,b-1]}R_{k}\right)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}R_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{j}\text{ for }j\in[a,b-1]\right\}

\displaystyle=K_{p_{a}}+p_{b}(B-A)

\displaystyle\leq K_{p_{a}}+K_{p_{b}}.

Here, the second inequality is by the monotonicity of the functions
g_{j}
for every
j\in[\ell]
and the last equality is by
B.1
.
Thus, all inequalities are equations and we have that
p_{b}(B-A)=K_{p_{b}}
, contradicting minimality of the set
B\in{\mathcal{F}}_{p_{b}}
.
∎

B.2
Optimizing over

Q

-polyhedron
in Strongly Polynomial Time

In this section, we prove
Lemma

5.13
. Let
p\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
and
m\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}V\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
be two functions and let
k\geq K_{p}
. We will require the following generalization of the polyhedron
Q(p,m)
from
[
8
]
.

\mathcal{Q}(p,k,m)\coloneqq\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{V}\ \middle|\begin{array}[]{l}{(\text{i})}{\ \ \ 0\leq x_{u}\leq\min\{1,m_{u}\}}\hfill{\qquad\forall\ u\in V}\\
{(\text{ii})}{\ \ x(Z)\geq 1}\hfill{\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \ \ \text{if }p(Z)=k}\\
{(\text{iii})}{\ x(u)=1}\hfill{\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \ \ \ \text{if }m(u)=k}\\
{(\text{iv})}{\ x(Z)\leq m(Z)-p(Z)+1}\hfill{\ \ \ \forall\ Z\subseteq V}\\
{(\text{v})}{\ \ \left\lfloor{\frac{m(V)}{k}}\right\rfloor\leq x(V)\leq\left\lceil{\frac{m(V)}{k}}\right\rceil}\hfill\\
\end{array}\right\}.

We note that
Q(p,m)=\mathcal{Q}(p,K_{p},m)
.
We will also require the following definitions and results from
[
30
,
31
]
.
Let
f\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a set function.
We define the following two polyhedra w.r.t the function
f
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(f)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{V}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x\geq 0,x(Z)\geq f(Z)\text{ for every }Z\subseteq V\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}(f)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{V}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x(V)=f(V),x(Z)\geq f(Z)\text{ for every }Z\subseteq V\right\}.

If the set function
f
is supermodular, then the polyhedron
\mathcal{C}(f)
is
called the
contrapolymatroid
with border function
f
. Furthermore, if the function
f
is also finite, then the polyhedron
\mathcal{B}(f)
is called the
base-contrapolymatroid
with border function
f
.
The following result by
[
30
]
shows properties of the polyhedron
\mathcal{C}(p)
whose border function
p\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{-\infty\}
is skew-supermodular.

Theorem B.1

(
[
30
]
)
.

Let
p\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{-\infty\}
be a skew-supermodular function. We have the following:

1.

for a given vector
c\in\mathbb{R}^{V}
,
an optimum solution to the problem
\min\{\sum_{u\in V}c_{u}x_{u}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x\in\mathcal{C}(p)\}
can be computed in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
, and

2.

there exists a unique supermodular function
{p}^{\uparrow}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{-\infty\}
such that
\mathcal{C}(p)=\mathcal{C}\left({p}^{\uparrow}\right)
.

A function
b^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
is
crossing-submodular
if
b^{\prime\prime}(A)+b^{\prime\prime}(B)\geq b^{\prime\prime}(A\cap B)+b^{\prime\prime}(A\cup B)
for every
A,B\subseteq V
for which the four sets
A-B,B-A,A\cap B
and
V-(A\cup B)
are non-empty.
For convenience, we define the
minimization oracle

{f}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
for a set function
f\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
as follows: for given disjoint sets
S_{0},T_{0}\subseteq V
and vector
y_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{V}
,
{f}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}(S_{0},T_{0},y_{0})
returns a set
Z
and its value
f(Z)
such that
S_{0}\subseteq Z\subseteq V-T_{0}
and
f(Z)-y_{0}(Z)\leq f(X)-y_{0}(X)
for every
S_{0}\subseteq X\subseteq V-T_{0}
(i.e., it returns the output of
{(-f)}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}(S_{0},T_{0},-y_{0})
).
The following result is implicit in
[
31
]
and says that given access to
{b^{\prime\prime}}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
of a crossing-submodular function
b^{\prime\prime}
, we can optimize over the polyhedron
\mathcal{B}(b^{\prime\prime})
in strongly polynomial time.

Theorem B.2

(
[
31
]
Section IV)
.

Let
b^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{-\infty\}
be a crossing-submodular function. Then, for a given vector
c\in\mathbb{R}^{V}
,
\arg\min\{\sum_{v\in V}c_{v}x_{v}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x\in\mathcal{B}(b^{\prime\prime})\}
can be computed in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{b^{\prime\prime}}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
.

We recall that our goal is to show
Lemma

5.13
which says that for a non-negative function
m\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}V\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and a function
p\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
defined as the maximum of two skew-supermodular functions,
we can optimize over the polyhedron
Q(p,m)

in strongly polynomial time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to the function maximization oracles of the individual skew-supermodular functions. As a step towards showing
Lemma

5.13
, we first show that we can optimize over the polyhedron
Q(p,m)
when the function
p
is itself a skew-supermodular function. In fact, we will show the following stronger statement that we can optimize over the polyhedra
\mathcal{Q}(p,k,m)
for every given positive integer
k\geq K_{p}
.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
p\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{-\infty\}
be a skew-supermodular function.
Then, the following optimization problem can be solved in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
: for a given non-negative function
m\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}V\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and a positive integer
k
such that
\mathcal{Q}(p,k,m)\neq\emptyset
and a given cost vector
c\in\mathbb{R}^{V}
, find an extreme point optimum solution to the following linear program:

\max\left\{\sum_{u\in V}c_{u}x_{u}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x\in\mathcal{Q}(p,k,m)\right\}.

Proof.

We consider the family
{\mathcal{F}}_{p,k}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\{Z\subseteq V\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}p(Z)=k\text{ but }p(Z^{\prime})<k\text{ for every }Z^{\prime}\subsetneq Z\}
. We note that if
k=K_{p}
, then
{\mathcal{F}}_{p,k}={\mathcal{F}}_{p}
, the family of minimal
p
-maximizers; furthermore, if
k>K_{p}
, then
{\mathcal{F}}_{p,k}=\emptyset
.
We let
R_{p,k}\coloneqq V-\cup_{X\in{\mathcal{F}}_{p,k}}X
denote the set of elements not contained in any set of the family
{\mathcal{F}}_{p,k}
. We define the family
{\mathcal{L}}_{p,k}\coloneqq{\mathcal{F}}_{p,k}\cup\{R_{p,k}\}
and let
L_{p,k}\coloneqq\{Z\subseteq U\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}U\in{\mathcal{L}}_{p,k}\}
denote the family of all subsets of the members of
{\mathcal{L}}_{p,k}
. We note that
{\mathcal{L}}_{p,k}
is the family of maximal sets in the family
L_{p,k}
.
For every
U\in{\mathcal{L}}_{p,k}
, we let
p^{\prime}_{U}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{U}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{-\infty\}
be the function defined as
p^{\prime}_{U}(Z)\coloneqq p(Z)-1
for every
Z\subseteq U
. We note that the function
p^{\prime}_{U}
is skew-supermodular for every
U\in{\mathcal{L}}_{p,k}
. Then, by
Theorem

B.1
, there exists a unique fully supermodular function
{(p^{\prime}_{U})}^{\uparrow}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{U}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{-\infty\}
such that
\mathcal{C}\left({(p^{\prime}_{U})}^{\uparrow}\right)=\mathcal{C}(p^{\prime}_{U})
. We will need the following claim which is implicit in
[
8
]
.

Claim B.2

(
[
8
, Proof of Lemma 2.4]
)
.

For every
U\in{\mathcal{L}}_{p,k}
and for every
Z\subseteq U
, we have that

{(p^{\prime}_{U})}^{\uparrow}(Z)=\min\left\{\sum_{u\in Z}x_{u}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x\in\mathcal{C}\left({(p^{\prime}_{U})}^{\uparrow}\right)\right\}.

The next claim says that an evaluation oracle for the function
{(p^{\prime}_{U})}^{\uparrow}
can be implemented in strongly polynomial time.

Claim B.3
.

For every
U\in{\mathcal{L}}_{p,k}
and each
Z\subseteq U
, the value
{(p^{\prime}_{U})}^{\uparrow}(Z)
can be evaluated in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
.

Proof.

Let
U\in{\mathcal{L}}_{p,k}
and
Z\subseteq U
. By
B.2
and
Theorem

B.1
, we have that

{(p^{\prime}_{U})}^{\uparrow}(Z)=\min\left\{\sum_{u\in Z}x_{u}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x\in\mathcal{C}\left({(p^{\prime}_{U})}^{\uparrow}\right)\right\}=\min\left\{\sum_{u\in Z}x_{u}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x\in\mathcal{C}\left(p^{\prime}_{U}\right)\right\}.

Thus, it suffices to solve the RHS optimization problem. We recall that the function
p^{\prime}_{U}
is skew-supermodular. Furthermore, we note that a query to
{p^{\prime}_{U}}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
can be implemented in
O(|V|)
time using one query to
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
. Then, by
Theorem

B.1
we can solve the RHS optimization problem
in
\poly(|V|)
time and
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
.
∎

We now define a central function that will allow us to optimize over the polyhedron
\mathcal{Q}(p,k,m)
. For convenience, we define this function in three stages using two intermediate functions. Let the first intermediate function
f_{p,k}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{-\infty\}
be defined as follows: for every
Z\subseteq V
,

f_{p,k}(Z)\coloneqq\begin{cases}{(p^{\prime}_{U})}^{\uparrow}(Z)&\text{ for every $Z\in L_{p,k}$,}\\
-\infty&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

For the second stage, we will require a larger ground set. Let
V^{\prime}\coloneqq V\uplus\{t\}
be the ground set extended with a new element
t
. Let the second intermediate function
b^{\prime}_{p,k,m}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{+\infty\}
be defined as follows: for every
Z\subseteq V^{\prime}
,

b^{\prime}_{p,k,m}(Z)\coloneqq\begin{cases}m(Z)-1&\text{ if $Z\in{\mathcal{F}}_{p,k}$}\\
+\infty&\text{ if $t\not\in Z$ and $Z\not\in{\mathcal{F}}_{p,k}$}\\
-f_{p,k}(V-Z)&\text{ if $t\in Z$}.\end{cases}

We now define our central function of interest. Let
{\mathcal{D}}_{m,k}\coloneqq\{u\in V\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}m(u)=k\}
. Let the function
b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{+\infty\}
be defined as follows: for every
Z\subseteq V^{\prime}
,

b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m}(Z)\coloneqq\begin{cases}b^{\prime}_{p,k,m}(Z)&\text{ if $Z$ is not a singleton or complement of a singleton}\\
\min\left\{b^{\prime}_{p,k,m}(u),m(u)-\mathbbm{1}_{u\in{\mathcal{D}}_{m,k}}\right\}&\text{ if $X=\{u\}\subseteq V$}\\
\min\left\{b^{\prime}_{p,k,m}(t),-m(V)+\left\lceil{m(V)/k}\right\rceil\right\}&\text{ if $X=\{t\}$}\\
\min\left\{b^{\prime}_{p,k,m}(Z),-m(u)+1\right\}&\text{ if $X=V^{\prime}-\{u\}$ for some $u\in V$}\\
\min\left\{b^{\prime}_{p,k,m}(V),m(V)-\left\lfloor{m(V)/k}\right\rfloor\right\}&\text{ if $X=V$}.\end{cases}

The next claim is also implicit in
[
8
]
and says that the projection of the base contrapolymatroid with border function
b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m}
onto the ground set
V
is a translated reflection of the polyhedron
\mathcal{Q}(p,k,m)
. We formally define the projection of the base contrapolymatroid onto the ground set
V
as
\mathtt{proj}_{V}(\mathcal{B}(b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m}))\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{V}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\exists x_{t}\in\mathbb{R}\text{ s.t. }(x,x_{t})\in\mathcal{B}(b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m})\}
.

Claim B.4

(
[
8
]
)
.

\mathtt{proj}_{V}(\mathcal{B}(b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m}))=m-\mathcal{Q}(p,k,m)
.

By
B.4
, it follows that if we can optimize over
\mathcal{B}(b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m})
in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{p}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
, then we can optimize over
\mathcal{Q}(p,k,m)
in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{p}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
.
We now show that we can optimize over the base-contrapolymatroid
\mathcal{B}(b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m})
in two steps.
Firstly, we show that the function
b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m}
is crossing-submodular. Thus, by
Theorem

B.2
, we can optimize over the base-contrapolymatroid
\mathcal{B}(b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m})
in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m}}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
.
Secondly, we show that
{b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m}}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
can be implemented in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to

{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
. This will complete the proof of the lemma.

We now show the first step, i.e. the function
b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m}
is crossing-submodular. We prove this as
B.6
after the next claim which is also implicit in
[
8
]
.

Claim B.5

(
[
8
, Proof of Lemma 2.4]
)
.

The function
b^{\prime}_{p,k,m}
is crossing-submodular.

Claim B.6
.

The function
b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m}
is crossing-submodular.

Proof.

The function
b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m}
is obtained from the function
b^{\prime}_{p,k,m}
by decreasing values on singletons and complement of singletons. This operation is known to preserve crossing submodularity (for example, see part (c) in Section 2 of
[
33
]
) and so the claim follows from
B.5
.
∎

We now show the second part, i.e.
{b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m}}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
can be implemented in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to

{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
.
Let the input to
{b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m}}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
be disjoint sets
A,B\subseteq V^{\prime}
and a vector
y\in\mathbb{R}^{V^{\prime}}
.
The goal is to answer
{b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m}}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}(A,B,y)
, i.e., we would like to find an optimum solution to
\min\{b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m}(Z)-y(Z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}A\subseteq Z\subseteq V^{\prime}-B\}
.
By definition of the function
b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m}
, it follows that if we can find an optimum solution to
\min\{-f_{p,k}(V-Z)+y(Z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}A\cup\{t\}\subseteq Z\subseteq V^{\prime}-B\}

in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to

{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
,
then we can find an optimum solution to
\min\{b^{\prime\prime}_{p,k,m}(Z)-y(Z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}A\subseteq Z\subseteq V^{\prime}-B\}

in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to

{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
.
We have that

\displaystyle\min

\displaystyle\left\{-f_{p,k}(V-Z)+y(Z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}A\cup\{t\}\subseteq Z\subseteq V^{\prime}-B\right\}

\displaystyle=\max\left\{f_{p,k}(Z)-y(V^{\prime}-Z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}B\subseteq Z\subseteq V^{\prime}-(A\cup\{t\})\right\}.

By definition of the function
f_{p,k}
, in order to solve the RHS optimization problem in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
, it suffices to solve the following problem in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
:

\displaystyle\max\left\{\max_{U\in{\mathcal{L}}_{p,k}}\left\{{(p^{\prime}_{U})}^{\uparrow}(Z)-y(U-Z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}B\subseteq Z\subseteq U-A\right\}\right\}.

We note that the family
{\mathcal{L}}_{p,k}={\mathcal{F}}_{p,k}\cup\{R_{p}\}
can be computed in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
: if
k=K_{p}
, then the family
{\mathcal{F}}_{p,k}={\mathcal{F}}_{p}
which can be computed by
Lemma

4.10
. Otherwise (i.e., if
k>K_{p}
) the family
{\mathcal{F}}_{p,k}=\emptyset
, and consequently,
{\mathcal{L}}_{p,k}=\{V\}
. Thus, in order to solve the above optimization problem
in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
, it suffices to solve the following optimization problem
in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}

for every
U\in{\mathcal{L}}_{p,k}
:

\max\left\{{(p^{\prime}_{U})}^{\uparrow}(Z)-y(U-Z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}B\subseteq Z\subseteq U-A\right\}.

We recall from
B.2
that the function
{(p^{\prime}_{U})}^{\uparrow}
is supermodular and we also have an evaluation oracle for this function that runs in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
by
B.3
. Thus, we can solve the above optimization problem for every
U\in{\mathcal{L}}_{p,k}
using supermodular maximization (i.e., submodular minimization) in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to the evaluation oracle of
{p^{\prime}_{U}}^{\uparrow}
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Armed with the ability to optimize over
\mathcal{Q}(p,k,m)
for skew-supermodular functions
p
, we now move on to show
Lemma

5.13
, i.e. optimizing over
Q(p,m)
when
p
is the maximum of two skew-supermodular functions. We will show that this reduces to optimizing over the intersection of two
\mathcal{Q}
-polyhedra which we already know how to optimize over by
Lemma

B.1
. We will need the following additional definitions and results from
[
30
]
.

Definition B.1
.

Let
p\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{-\infty\}
and
b\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{+\infty\}
be two set functions. The pair
(p,b)
is
paramodular
(or is a
strong pair
) if
p(\emptyset)=b(\emptyset)=0
, the function
p
is supermodular, the function
b
is submodular, and the functions
p
and
b
satisfy the
cross-inequality
:
b(X)-p(Y)\geq b(X-Y)-p(Y-X)
for every pair of subsets
X,Y\subseteq V
. If
(p,b)
is paramodular, then the polyhedren

\mathcal{G}(p,b)\coloneqq\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{V}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}p(Z)\leq x(Z)\leq b(Z)\text{ for every $Z\subseteq V$}\}

is said to be a
generalized polymatroid
(or
g-polymatroid
) with
border pair

(p,b)
. Here,
p
is the lower border function and
b
is the upper border function.

The following theorem says that a g-polymatroid uniquely determines its border paramodular pair.

Theorem B.3

(
[
30
, Theorem 14.2.8]
)
.

Let
Q\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{V}
be a g-polymatroid. Then, there exists a unique
paramodular pair
(p\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{-\infty\},b\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{+\infty\})
such that
Q=\mathcal{G}(p,b)
. Furthermore, for every
Z\subseteq V
,
b(Z)=\max\{x(Z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}z\in Q\}
and
p(Z)=\min\{x(Z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x\in Q\}
.

We will also need the following theorem by
[
31
]
which says that for a crossing-submodular function, there exists a unique fully submodular function that realizes the same base polymatroid and can be evaluated in strongly polynomial time given access to a function minimization oracle.

Theorem B.4

(
[
31
, Section IV.4]
)
.

Let
b\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
be a crossing submodular function. We have the following:

(1)

if
\mathcal{B}(b)
is non-empty, then there exists a unique fully submodular function
{b}^{\downarrow\downarrow}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{+\infty\}
such that
\mathcal{B}(b)=\mathcal{B}\left({b}^{\downarrow\downarrow}\right)
, and

(2)

{b}^{\downarrow\downarrow}(Z)
can be computed in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{b}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
.

We now restate and prove
Lemma

5.13
.
See
5.13

Proof.

We note that
K_{p}=\max\{K_{q},K_{r}\}
. Consequently,
Q(p,m)=\mathcal{Q}(p,K_{p},m)=\mathcal{Q}(q,K_{p},m)\cap\mathcal{Q}(r,K_{p},m)
. Thus, it suffices to optimize over the intersection of the polyhedra
\mathcal{Q}(q,K_{p},m)
and
\mathcal{Q}(r,K_{p},m)
both of which are non-empty.
The following claim from
[
8
]
says that the polyhedra
\mathcal{Q}(r,K_{p},m)
and
\mathcal{Q}(q,K_{p},m)
are g-polymatroids.

Claim B.7

(
[
8
, Lemma 2.4]
)
.

Let
p\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{-\infty\}
be a skew-supermodular function with
K_{p}>0
and
m\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}V\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
be a non-negative function. Then, for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
such that
k\geq K_{p}
, the polyhedron
\mathcal{Q}(p,k,m)
is a g-polymatroid.

Thus, by
B.7
and
Theorem

B.3
, there exist functions
p_{1},p_{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{-\infty\}
and
b_{1},b_{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{+\infty\}
such that
\mathcal{Q}(q,K_{p},m)=\mathcal{G}(p_{1},b_{1})
and
\mathcal{Q}(r,K_{p},m)=\mathcal{G}(p_{2},b_{2})
. By
Lemma

B.1
, we can optimize over the polyhedra
\mathcal{Q}(q,K_{p},m)
and
\mathcal{Q}(r,K_{p},m)
in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{q}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{wc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
and
{r}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{wc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
respectively. Thus, by
Theorem

B.3
, we can implement the evaluation oracle for the functions
p_{1},p_{2},b_{1},b_{2}
in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{q}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{wc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
and
{r}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{wc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
.

Let
V^{\prime}\coloneqq V^{1}\uplus V^{2}
denote an extended ground set, where
V^{1}
and
V^{2}
are disjoint copies of the ground set
V
. We define the function
b\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{+\infty\}
as follows: for every
Z\subseteq V
,

b(Z)\coloneqq\begin{cases}b_{1}(Z)&\text{ if $Z\subseteq V^{1}$}\\
-p_{1}(Z)&\text{ if $V^{\prime}-Z\subseteq V^{1}$ }\\
b_{2}(Z)&\text{ if $Z\subseteq V^{2}$}\\
-p_{2}(Z)&\text{ if $V^{\prime}-Z\subseteq V^{2}$}\\
+\infty&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

The function
b
can be shown to be crossing submodular (implicit in
[
30
]
).

Claim B.8

(
[
30
, Proof of Theorem 16.1.7]
)
.

The function
b
is crossing submodular.

Thus, by
Theorem

B.4
(1), there exists a unique fully-submodular function
{b}^{\downarrow\downarrow}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{+\infty\}
such that
\mathcal{B}(b)=\mathcal{B}\left({b}^{\downarrow\downarrow}\right)
. Using the results in
[
30
]
, it can be shown that
\mathcal{Q}(q,K_{p},m)\cap\mathcal{Q}(r,K_{p},m)
is
in fact a
sub-flow
polyhedron
constrained
by the function
b
. This allows us to optimize over
\mathcal{Q}(r,K_{p},m)\cap\mathcal{Q}(q,K_{p},m)
in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{{b}^{\downarrow\downarrow}}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
.
We note that that we will not require any technical details regarding subflow polyhedra beyond this connection and so we refrain from defining subflow polyhedra. We refer the reader to
[
30
, Section 16.1]
for an extensive survey of the topic.

Claim B.9

(
[
30
, Section 16.3.2]
)
.

For a given vector
c\in\mathbb{R}^{V}
, we can find an optimum solution to
\min\{\sum_{u\in V}c_{u}x_{u}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x\in\mathcal{Q}(q,K_{p},m)\cap\mathcal{Q}(r,K_{p},m)\}
in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{{b}^{\downarrow\downarrow}}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
.

Thus, by
B.9
, it suffices to implement
{{b}^{\downarrow\downarrow}}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{q}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{wc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
and
{r}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{wc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
.
We note that since the function
{b}^{\downarrow\downarrow}
is submodular,
{{b}^{\downarrow\downarrow}}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
can be
implemented in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to the function evaluation oracle of
{b}^{\downarrow\downarrow}
(via submodular minimization).
By
Theorem

B.4
(2), the function evaluation oracle of
{b}^{\downarrow\downarrow}
can be implemented in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{b}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
.
By definition of the function
b
,
{b}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
can be implemented in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{b_{1}}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
,
{b_{2}}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
,
{(-p_{1})}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
, and
{(-p_{2})}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
. We note that
{b_{1}}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
,
{b_{2}}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
,
{(-p_{1})}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
, and
{(-p_{2})}\text{-min-}\mathtt{Oracle}
can be implemented in
\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to respective function evaluation oracles via submodular minimization since
b_{1},b_{2}
are submodular functions and
p_{1},p_{2}
are supermodular functions. We recall that the evaluation oracles for
p_{1},p_{2},b_{1},b_{2}
can be implemented in

\poly(|V|)
time using
\poly(|V|)
queries to
{q}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{wc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
and
{r}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{wc}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

B.3
Computing
\alpha^{(4)}
: Ratio Maximization Oracle in Strongly Polynomial Time

In this section, we prove
Lemma

5.14
. We will need the following general result that is implicit in the work of Cunningham
[
23
]
. We include a proof of the result for completeness.

Proposition B.1

(
[
23
]
)
.

Let
f\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
,
g\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{+}
be two functions given by evaluation oracles. Given an oracle that takes
\lambda\in\mathbb{Q}
as input and returns
\arg\max\{f(X)-\lambda g(X)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}X\subseteq V\}
, there exists an algorithm that runs in time
O(K_{g})
and returns
\arg\max\left\{\frac{f(X)}{g(X)}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}X\subseteq V\right\}
, where
K_{g}\coloneqq\max\{g(X)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}X\subseteq V\}
.

Proof.

We will show that
Algorithm

3

(
Algorithm

3
is a generalization of Algorithm 1 of
[
23
]
) satisfies the claimed property.
Since the input function
g
is a positive function, we have that
\max\{f(X)/g(X)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}X\subseteq V\}=\lambda
if and only if
\max\left\{f(X)-\lambda g(X)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}X\subseteq V\right\}=0
. If
Algorithm

3
terminates, then for the set
Z
returned by the algorithm and for
\lambda\coloneqq f(Z)/g(Z)
, we have that
f(Z)-\lambda g(Z)=0
by definition of
\lambda
and
\max\{f(X)-\lambda g(X)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}X\subseteq V\}\leq 0
by termination condition of the algorithm, which together imply that
\max\{f(X)-\lambda g(X)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}X\subseteq V\}=0
. Consequently, if
Algorithm

3
terminates, then the set
Z
returned by the algorithm
indeed maximizes the ratio
f(X)/g(X)
.

Algorithm 3

Ratio Maximization

\mathtt{Algorithm}(f,g)
:

1:

Z\coloneqq V
,
\lambda\coloneqq f(V)/g(V)

2:

while

\max\left\{f(X)-\lambda g(X)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}X\subseteq V\right\}>0

do

3:

Z\coloneqq\arg\max\left\{f(X)-\lambda g(X)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}X\subseteq V\right\}

4:

\lambda\coloneqq f(Z)/g(Z)

5:

return
Z

Consider an arbitrary while-loop iteration of
Algorithm

3
in which
\max\{f(X)-\lambda g(X)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}X\subseteq V\}>0
. Let
\lambda^{\prime}
and
Z^{\prime}
denote by the value
\lambda
and the set
Z\subseteq V
in the subsequent while-loop iteration of the algorithm. Furthermore, let
\lambda^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathbb{Z}^{\prime\prime}
denote the value
\lambda
and the set
Z\subseteq V
after two while-loop iterations of the algorithm. In particular, we have that

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\arg\max\{f(X)-\lambda g(X)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}X\subseteq V\},

\displaystyle\lambda^{\prime}

\displaystyle\coloneqq f(Z^{\prime})/g(Z^{\prime}),

\displaystyle Z^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\arg\max\{f(X)-\lambda^{\prime}g(X)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}X\subseteq V\},

\displaystyle\lambda^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\coloneqq f(Z^{\prime\prime})/g(Z^{\prime\prime}).

Then the following claim says that either the algorithm returns the set
Z^{\prime\prime}
, or
g(Z^{\prime})<g(Z^{\prime\prime})
.

Claim B.10
.

Either
f(Z^{\prime\prime})-\lambda^{\prime}g(Z^{\prime\prime})=0
or
g(Z^{\prime\prime})>g(Z^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Suppose that
f(Z^{\prime\prime})-\lambda^{\prime}g(Z^{\prime\prime})>0
. Then, we have the following:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle<f(Z^{\prime\prime})-\lambda^{\prime}g(Z^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=f(Z^{\prime\prime})-\lambda g(Z^{\prime\prime})+\lambda g(Z^{\prime\prime})-\lambda^{\prime}g(Z^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\leq f(Z^{\prime})-\lambda g(Z^{\prime})+\lambda g(Z^{\prime\prime})-\lambda^{\prime}g(Z^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\lambda^{\prime}g(Z^{\prime})-\lambda g(Z^{\prime})+\lambda g(Z^{\prime\prime})-\lambda^{\prime}g(Z^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=(\lambda^{\prime}-\lambda)(g(Z^{\prime})-g(Z^{\prime\prime}))

\displaystyle<g(Z^{\prime})-g(Z^{\prime\prime})

Here, the second inequality is by definition of the set
Z^{\prime}
. The second equality is by the definition of the value
\lambda^{\prime}
. The final inequality is because
\lambda<f(Z^{\prime})/g(Z^{\prime})=\lambda^{\prime}
.
∎

By
B.10
, we have that during every iteration of the while-loop, the value
g(Z^{\prime})>g(Z)
. Since the range of the function
g
is the set of positive integers, the number of while-loop iterations of the algorithm cannot exceed
K_{g}
.
∎

See
5.14

Proof.

For every pair of vertices
u,v\in{A\choose 2}
, we consider
f_{uv}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V-\{u,v\}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
and
g_{u,v}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}2^{V-\{u,v\}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{+}
defined as follows:

\displaystyle f_{uv}(Z)

\displaystyle\coloneqq p\left(Z\cup\left\{u,v\right\}\right)-y\left(Z\cup\left\{u,v\right\}\right)\ \ \text{for every $Z\subseteq V-\{u,v\}$,}

\displaystyle g_{uv}(Z)

\displaystyle\coloneqq 1+\left|\left(A-\left\{u,v\right\}\right)\cap Z\right|\ \ \text{for every $Z\subseteq V-\{u,v\}$.}

Then, we have that

\max_{u,v\in\binom{A}{2}}\left\{\max\left\{\frac{f_{uv}(Z)}{g_{uv}(Z)}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}Z\subseteq V-\{u,v\}\right\}\right\}=\max\left\{\frac{p(Z)-y(Z)}{|A\cap Z|-1}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}|A\cap Z|\geq 2,Z\subseteq V\right\}.

Moreover, for a pair
u,v\in\binom{A}{2}
and a set
Z\subseteq V-\{u,v\}
that achieves the maximum in the LHS, the set
Z\cup\{u,v\}
achieves the maximum in the RHS. Hence, it suffices to solve the optimization problem

\arg\max\left\{\frac{f_{uv}(Z)}{g_{uv}(Z)}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}Z\subseteq V-\{u,v\}\right\}

for every pair
u,v\in{A\choose 2}
. Fix a pair
u,v\in{A\choose 2}
. By
Proposition

B.1
, we can solve
the above optimization problem in strongly polynomial time given access to evaluation oracles for
f_{uv}
and
g_{uv}
, and the maximization oracle
\arg\max\left\{f_{uv}(Z)-\lambda g_{uv}(Z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}Z\subseteq V-\{u,v\}\right\}
for given
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}
. We note that the value
g_{uv}(Z)
can be computed explicitly in time
O(|V|)
. Furthermore, the value
f_{uv}(Z)
can be computed by querying
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
with inputs

S_{0}=Z\cup\{u,v\}
,
T_{0}=V-(Z\cup\{u,v\})
, and using the same
y_{0}
. Finally, the maximization oracle
\arg\max\left\{f_{uv}(Z)-\lambda g_{uv}(Z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}Z\subseteq V-\{u,v\}\right\}
for a given
\lambda
can be implemented in
\poly(|V|)
time by querying
{p}\text{-}\mathtt{max}\text{-}\mathtt{Oracle}
with inputs
S_{0}=\{u,v\}
,
T_{0}=\emptyset
, and
y_{0}=-\lambda\chi_{A-\{u,v\}}-y
.

∎
